At work, and sometimes even at home, I get so mixed up I’ll just stand there like my hands are still on the forklift controls. And I’ll repeat the same safety phrase over and over, even when people are trying to talk to me. That’s not really like me.\n\nDoctor: Do these voices and messages from the scanners and dispatch seem to be all part of one big story or plan to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It feels like it’s all connected. The routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. I feel like it’s marking my moves, and the scanners are sending messages through it… like dispatch is trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself jumping from one topic to another when talking, without really finishing your thoughts?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t notice myself doing that much, but maybe other people would. I feel like I usually try to answer what’s being asked, though sometimes I lose track a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disorganized or hard to put into words when these voices are happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it disorganized. When it’s happening, it’s harder to get the words out cleanly. I can feel stuck or lose what I was about to say, but I’m not always aware of it in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected from each other when these voices are not happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. When the voices aren’t going on, my thoughts feel more normal to me. I don’t really notice them being disconnected, but I might still get tired or lose my place sometimes.\n\nDoctor: When the voices are not happening, do you have trouble keeping your thoughts in order?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. When the voices aren’t happening, it’s usually easier for me to think, but I can still get a little mixed up or lose my place. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. [
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because things at work don’t feel right anymore. The routing system at the warehouse—it’s not just a computer to me. I know it’s been tracking my moves and sending messages through the scanners, like it’s trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nDoctor: Have you actually heard voices or messages from the scanners, or is it more of a feeling that they're trying to communicate with you?\n\nPatient: No, it’s not just a feeling. I hear them. Like even when I’m sitting out in my garage and everything’s shut off, I can hear dispatch voices clear as day. They’ll call my operator number and say I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me. It sounds real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to follow your own thoughts or to express them to others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I can usually say what I’m trying to say, I think. Sometimes I lose my place when I’m upset, but I haven’t really noticed my thoughts getting tangled up or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing these dispatch voices?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on for about a year and eight months now. Around then is when I first started hearing the dispatch voices and thinking the scanners were sending those messages.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting in ways that are out of character for you since these voices started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. At work, and sometimes even at home, I get so mixed up I’ll just stand there like my hands are still on the forklift controls. And I’ll repeat the same safety phrase over and over, even when people are trying to talk to me. That’s not really like me.\n\nDoctor: Do these voices and messages from the scanners and dispatch seem to be all part of one big story or plan to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It feels like it’s all connected. The routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. I feel like it’s marking my moves, and the scanners are sending messages through it… like dispatch is trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself jumping from one topic to another when talking, without really finishing your thoughts?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t notice myself doing that much, but maybe other people would. I feel like I usually try to answer what’s being asked, though sometimes I lose track a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disorganized or hard to put into words when these voices are happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it disorganized. When it’s happening, it’s harder to get the words out cleanly. I can feel stuck or lose what I was about to say, but I’m not always aware of it in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected from each other when these voices are not happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. When the voices aren’t going on, my thoughts feel more normal to me. I don’t really notice them being disconnected, but I might still get tired or lose my place sometimes.\n\nDoctor: When the voices are not happening, do you have trouble keeping your thoughts in order?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. When the voices aren’t happening, it’s usually easier for me to think, but I can still get a little mixed up or lose my place. I don’t know if that means my thoughts aren’t in order, though.\n\nDoctor: When the voices are not happening, do you feel like your thoughts are disjointed or don't make sense to you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. When the voices aren’t there, my thoughts usually make sense to me. I might lose track a bit or need a second, but I wouldn’t say they feel disjointed.\n\nDoctor: When you're hearing the dispatch voices, do your thoughts feel like they're all over the place or hard to follow?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say all over the place. When the dispatch voices are happening, it’s definitely harder to keep track of what I’m thinking, because I’m listening to them or trying not to. My thoughts can get interrupted, but I don’t know if they’re hard to follow in the way you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

When the voices aren’t there, my thoughts usually make sense to me. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because things at work don’t feel right anymore. The routing system at the warehouse—it’s not just a computer to me. I know it’s been tracking my moves and sending messages through the scanners, like it’s trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nDoctor: Have you actually heard voices or messages from the scanners, or is it more of a feeling that they're trying to communicate with you?\n\nPatient: No, it’s not just a feeling. I don’t know if that means my thoughts aren’t in order, though.\n\nDoctor: When the voices are not happening, do you feel like your thoughts are disjointed or don't make sense to you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. When the voices aren’t there, my thoughts usually make sense to me. I might lose track a bit or need a second, but I wouldn’t say they feel disjointed.\n\nDoctor: When you're hearing the dispatch voices, do your thoughts feel like they're all over the place or hard to follow?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say all over the place. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because things at work don’t feel right anymore. The routing system at the warehouse—it’s not just a computer to me. I know it’s been tracking my moves and sending messages through the scanners, like it’s trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nDoctor: Have you actually heard voices or messages from the scanners, or is it more of a feeling that they're trying to communicate with you?\n\nPatient: No, it’s not just a feeling. I hear them. Like even when I’m sitting out in my garage and everything’s shut off, I can hear dispatch voices clear as day. They’ll call my operator number and say I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me. It sounds real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to follow your own thoughts or to express them to others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I can usually say what I’m trying to say, I think. Sometimes I lose my place when I’m upset, but I haven’t really noticed my thoughts getting tangled up or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing these dispatch voices?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on for about a year and eight months now. Around then is when I first started hearing the dispatch voices and thinking the scanners were sending those messages.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting in ways that are out of character for you since these voices started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. At work, and sometimes even at home, I get so mixed up I’ll just stand there like my hands are still on the forklift controls. And I’ll repeat the same safety phrase over and over, even when people are trying to talk to me. That’s not really like me.\n\nDoctor: Do these voices and messages from the scanners and dispatch seem to be all part of one big story or plan to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It feels like it’s all connected. The routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. I feel like it’s marking my moves, and the scanners are sending messages through it… like dispatch is trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself jumping from one topic to another when talking, without really finishing your thoughts?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t notice myself doing that much, but maybe other people would. I feel like I usually try to answer what’s being asked, though sometimes I lose track a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disorganized or hard to put into words when these voices are happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it disorganized.
